Failure to do so will incur a 2min penalty for illegal equipment"* — a skater rule only, with no goalkeeper counterpart in the document), and for the carry-forward of Rules 18, 37, 38, 69.1 and 84 with Appendix 1 and the Section 6 fighting provision. **It contains no amendment to Rule 81, Rule 83 or Rule 26**, and no mention of the trapezoid or the Elite League · [England Ice Hockey Rules & Regulations 2024-2025](https://englandicehockey.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/09/1.0-5.0-EIH-Rules-and-Regulations.pdf) — Rule 22.3, the suspension of the Goalkeepers' Restricted Area · [USA Hockey Official Playing Rules 2025-29 (PDF)](https://cdn2.sportngin.com/attachments/document/945a-3442848/2025-29_USAH_Playing_Rules.pdf) — Rules 201–205 (including **204(b)**, read 31 August 2026 — *"shall always be at the players' bench and out of the play before any change is made"*, which writes no distance at all, and which adds the trigger *"or causes their team to gain a competitive advantage"* that no other book here writes — **204(c)** and its exception *"when a major or match penalty is imposed in Adult Classifications under Rule 403(d) or 405(a)"*, **205(a)**, whose premature goalkeeper substitution carries *"no time penalty"*, and **204(a)**, read in full on 31 August 2026 for the line-change procedure, the close of the change window and its warn-then-minor delay paragraph — the running head *"Teams"* is spliced between *"the Referee shall warn"* and *"the offending team"* in the extraction, so a whole-phrase grep of that sentence returns nothing), 401, 402–405, **407(c)** (the goalkeeper beyond the centre red line — *"If a goalkeeper deliberately participates in the play in any manner when they are beyond the center red line, a minor penalty shall be assessed"*; the rule prints no determining-factor sentence of its own, which is why the *Casebook* is cited for it below), 411, **607 (Charging)** — read in full for the goalkeeper clauses: **607(c)**, the minor-plus-misconduct or major-plus-game-misconduct for body checking or charging a goalkeeper *"within the goal crease or privileged area"*, **607(d)**'s "not fair game" protection outside it, and its Note 1 (accidental or unavoidable contact routed to the Interference rule, deliberate contact to charging) and Note 2 (the privileged-area construction) — 411, **615 (Fighting)** — read in full for a counterpart to the NHL's minor for leaving the immediate vicinity of the crease during an altercation, and it carries one at **615(e)**: a minor on a player who *"fails to move immediately and directly to their players' bench (or goal crease for a goalkeeper) when instructed to do so by the Referee during an altercation"*, with **615(d)** adding a game misconduct on *"any player or goalkeeper who is the first to intervene in an altercation then in progress."* The book **names** that clause too: its **Summary of Penalties** lists *"615(e) Leaving crease area during altercation"* under Goalkeeper Infractions, and *"615(e) Players not going to bench after warning during altercation"* under Minor Penalty Personal Fouls. What USA Hockey does not use is the NHL's phrasing — *"leaving the crease"*, *"leaving their crease"* and *"leaving the immediate vicinity"* all return nothing against the playing rules, raw and whitespace-flattened, and its own goalkeeper-penalty rule 407 has only its three clauses — but that is a difference of wording and construction, not of whether the offence exists. The *Casebook* situations cited below **apply** 615(e); they do not supply it. Also Rules 603 (Boarding, and its (a), (b) and (c) severity limbs), 604, 605, 606, 607, 608, 609, 610 (Delaying the Game — 610(b), the goalkeeper who *"has an opportunity to play the puck prior to being pressured … but instead intentionally causes a stoppage of play"*, and 610(c), a minor on any player or goalkeeper who *"delays the game by deliberately shooting or batting the puck outside the playing area, including after a stoppage of play"*, and **610(g)**, the bench minor against a team causing delay by making additional substitutions, read 31 August 2026 — **and note the lettering**, because the *Casebook* cites this provision as *"610(h)"* at Situation 15 and elsewhere, and both volumes' Summary of Penalties uses *"610(e)"* for what the body prints as 610(d): the printed rule runs (a) to (g) and the citations above are the **body's** lettering), 611, 613, 614, 615, 617 (including 617(c)(4), the two limbs on which a goal is disallowed), 618 (Handling Puck with Hands — 618(a), the closed hand not immediately dropped, which is a stoppage and a last-play face-off; the minor attached instead to picking the puck up off the ice; and the penalty shot, optional minor and awarded goal where a defending player does it in the goal crease), 620, 621, 622, 623, 624, 625 (625(b), the flat crease bar, its neutral-zone face-off and its push-in relief; 625(a)(8), the minor, which requires physical contact), 628, 630, 631 (631(d), *"if the puck is accidentally shot or deflected out of play … a last play face-off will occur in the zone where the puck was shot or the deflection occurred"*), 634, 635, 636, 638, 639, 640, the Declaration of Player Safety and Standard of Play front matter — read 1 September 2026 for the charging bullet under *Standard of Play and Rule Emphasis – Body Checking*, whose *Enforcement Standard* reads *"A player cannot take more than two strides or travel an excessive distance to accelerate through a body check for the purpose of punishing the opponent"* and then lists among its examples *"[a]ccelerating through a check for the purpose of punishing the opponent"* and *"[s]kating a great distance for the purpose of delivering a check with excessive force"*, **neither of which carries a stride count** — and **Appendix V, the Glossary, read in full** — for the entries "Time-Out (Curfew Definition)" and "Altercation", and to establish that it carries no entry for "player" and none for "skater". The *Casebook* was searched alongside it for an interpretive provision on whether a bare "player" reaches a goalkeeper; neither volume has one · [Hockey Canada Playing Rules 2026-2028 (PDF)](https://cdn.hockeycanada.ca/hockey-canada/Hockey-Programs/Officiating/Downloads/2026-28-hc-rulebook-e.pdf) — Rules 4.8 (and Interpretation 2), **4.13(c) and its Interpretation 2**, **6.1 — read 31 August 2026 for 6.1(a)'s *"at the Players' Bench (within 3.05m (10 ft.)) and out of play before any change is made"* and 6.1(b)'s penalty-bench change, which 6.1(b) prices as *"a Minor penalty under Rule 10.4 (a) – Leaving the Players' or Penalty Bench"* rather than as a bench minor — with **10.7(a) and its Interpretation 3**, which assesses too many players where the goaltender or their replacement plays the puck *"regardless of whether or not the goaltender is within the 3.05 m (10 ft.) area"***, **6.3(e)(i) and its Note 1** — *"Referees must penalize any player of either team who deliberately shoots, bats, or throws the puck outside the playing surface, as per Rule 10.1 (ii) – Delay of Game"*, the book's own cross-reference to that limb, carrying no goaltender exclusion — and **6.4(a)**, which does carry one for a materially similar act; the five express exclusions were located by searching the whole book, de-hyphenated and whitespace-flattened, for *"excluding the goaltender"* and *"excluding goaltenders"*, and they sit at 2.4(b) Note 2, 6.2, 6.4(a), 6.7(e)(i) and 10.2. Then 6.7 (and Interpretations 1, 5 and 8), 6.11 (and its thirteen Interpretations), and **Rule 7.10(a), 7.10(d), 7.10(e)(ii), 7.10(f) and Interpretation 1 to Rule 7.10(a)**, which between them are Hockey Canada's answer on the goaltender and a crease-side fight. The offence is in the rule text: 7.10(a) reads *"A Minor penalty will be assessed to a goaltender who leaves their crease during a fight. This should be recorded on the Official Game Report as 'Leaving the Crease'."* 7.10(d) supplies the neutral-location route — *"The goaltenders must stay in their goal creases or proceed to a neutral zone designated by the Referee."* 7.10(e)(ii) assesses a game misconduct to a player who *"joins in a fight or acts as a peacemaker"*, which the **Glossary** definition of *"PLAYERS"* carries to a goaltender — *"The goaltender is considered a player, except where explicitly stated because special rules or privileges apply to them"* — 7.10(e) stating no such exception. Interpretation 1 then answers the crease-side fight only: asked whether the goaltender may go to the Players' Bench where *"a fight occurs in the immediate vicinity of the goal crease"*, it answers *"With the permission of the Referee, the goaltender may proceed to their bench. If they do so on their own accord, they are to be assessed a Minor penalty for leaving their crease during a fight"* — **the strictest of the four on the narrow question of moving before being told to**, where the NHL and IIHF direct the referee to assess no penalty, and USA Hockey's *Casebook* Situation 26 penalises only a goalkeeper who both lacked permission and *"gets involved in the altercation outside of the crease"*, its Situation 27 answering a flat *"Yes."* **Rule 4.13(c) is the goaltender beyond the centre red line, and it is the one of the four that carries no penalty in its own text**: a goaltender who *"intentionally participates in the play in any manner when they are beyond the centre red line"* *"will be penalized under Rule 8.3 (a) – Interference"*, which was read too and assesses *"A Minor penalty … to any player who interferes with an opposing player."* **Interpretation 2 to Rule 4.13(c)** supplies the determining factor and reverses the NHL's: on a goaltender whose skates are over the line playing a puck still on their own side, *"the determining factor is the position of the goaltender's skates and not the position of the puck. Assess the goaltender a Minor penalty for Rule 8.3 (a) – Interference."* ⚠️ **Rule 6.11(b) carries two separate i./ii. lists, so "6.11(b)(ii)" names two different rules**: the **tag-up clause** sits under *"The Linesperson will drop their arm to nullify the off-side violation and allow play to continue if"* and requires that *"all attacking players clear the attacking zone by making skate contact with the blue-line, so that the attacking zone is completely clear of attacking players"*; the **stop-play clause** sits under *"During a delayed offside, if"* and reaches only an attacker who *"touches the puck or attempts to gain possession of a loose puck while the puck is still in the attacking zone"*, with no pressure limb and no contact limb. The disallowed goal is the first item of that second list, with its Note 1; the intentional off-side is 6.11(d). Rule 6.11 carries no category or division clause anywhere. Then 6.17, 7.1 (and Interpretations 1 and 2), 7.2, 7.3, 7.4 (and Interpretations 1 and 2), 7.5 (and Interpretations 1, 2 and 3), 7.6 (and Interpretation 2), 7.7 (and Interpretations 1 and 3), 7.8, 7.9, 8.1, 8.2 (and Interpretation 1), 8.5 (Interference with the Goaltender, and its Interpretations 1, 2 and 3 with Note 1; its opening paragraph, which acknowledges that *"incidental contact with the goaltender may occur"* and puts the onus on the attacking player; its *Protection of the Goaltender* paragraph, which reaches unnecessary contact *"anywhere on the ice"*; and its penalty limbs 8.5(a)–(e) — the minor at (a); at **(b)** a major and game misconduct at the referee's discretion *"based on the degree of violence of impact"* and mandatorily — *"will be assessed"* — for a player who *"charges the goaltender"* (Note 1 referring the definition to Rule 7.4(b) Interpretation 2) and for one who *"injures an opponent by an Interference with the Goaltender infraction that would otherwise call for a Minor penalty"*; at **(c)** a match penalty; at **(d)** the bar on any misconduct; at **(e)** the automatic game misconduct on any 8.5(b) major), 8.6 (and Interpretations 1 and 2), 8.7 (and Interpretation 1), 8.8 (and Interpretation 1), 9.1 (and Interpretation 1), 9.2 (and Interpretations 1 and 2), 9.3, 9.4, 9.5 (and its Interpretations), 3.2 (Lost or Broken Sticks, and Interpretation 1 to 3.2(c)), 10.1 (Delay of Game — its preamble, which reaches a player or team who *"deliberately causes a stoppage of play or delays the restart of play"*; 10.1(ii), a player who *"deliberately shoots, bats, or throws the puck outside the playing surface"*; 10.1(v), a goaltender *"who shoots or bats the puck directly out of the playing surface, with their stick"*, which carries no deliberateness word of its own; and 10.1(vi), *"A goaltender who deliberately drops or throws the puck onto the top or back of the goal netting causing a stoppage of play"* — all thirteen numbered limbs and the unnumbered Players' Bench paragraph were read, and (ii) and (vi) are the only two that say *"throws"*, (ii) alone reaching a puck thrown out of the playing surface; and 10.1(a), the minor on a player who *"in the opinion of the Referee, is deliberately delaying the game"*. All twelve of its Interpretations were read — every one of them written to 10.1(a) — and none of them is on the question whether 10.1(v) reaches a goaltender who puts the puck out without meaning to; Interpretation 3 is about freezing the puck, and **Interpretation 5**, the only one whose subject is this limb — *"Clarifications as it pertains to goaltenders shooting or batting the puck directly out of play"* — gives the goaltender's escapes as the absence of glass, a puck that *"touches the glass or deflects off a player or official"*, and a rink whose two ends are not similar, and not the absence of intent), 10.5, 10.6, 10.7, 11.1, and the Glossary definition of "altercation" (also at Rule 7.10); read for the offside, icing, checking-from-behind, head-contact, goaltender-interference and tripping comparisons above. **Both the USA Hockey and Hockey Canada books were also searched in full for a provision on screening the goaltender or obstructing the goaltender's vision**: USA Hockey has none — its 625(a)(1) screen is one set on a skater — and Hockey Canada's only two, in Rule 8.5's crease paragraph and in Interpretation 3, both reach a player in the crease · [USA Hockey Official Rules and Casebook 2025-29 (PDF)](https://cdn3.sportngin.com/attachments/document/4f1a-3442850/2025-29_USAH_Playing_Rules___Casebook.pdf) — Casebook situations for **Rule 204, Situations 7, 8, 10 and 11**, read 31 August 2026 — 7 binds goalkeepers to the same *"at the bench"* test as everyone else; 8 penalises a change in which neither player played the puck or made contact because *"Team A gained a territorial or positional advantage as a result of the change"*; 10 keeps a youth team short-handed until the penalised player has left the penalty bench by way of the ice; 11 lets an adult team send a player straight from the players' bench after a major or match — and **Rule 205, Situation 1**, which calls the goalkeeper who turns back to defend after their replacement is in the play *"too many players on the ice"* rather than premature substitution; and situations for Rules 301, **407**, 601, 602, 604, 605, **607**, **608** — including **Situation 5**, which allows that a goalkeeper outside the privileged area *"can be legally checked"* while permitting an attacker to *"physically engage"* only *"When the goalkeeper has possession of the puck"* and requiring a penalty for *"any avoidable contact"* once they are no longer in control of it, and **Situation 6**, which puts the privileged area as extending *"to the closer end boards"* — and **Rule 608, Situation 1**, read 31 August 2026, which confines the minor plus misconduct to two named situations and lists the three instances in which the major plus game misconduct or the match penalty *"must be called"* — **613**, 615, 620, 623, 624, **625**, 630 and 639 — including **Rule 613, Situations 13 and 15**, the line-change violations, read 31 August 2026 — including all seventeen under **Rule 625**, read in full for a screening or goaltender-vision provision, of which they have none, and among them **Situation 9**, which supplies the limit on 625(b)'s goalkeeper-out carve-out: the goal stands where the goalkeeper is *"completely out of their crease"*, *"However, if the goalkeeper is attempting to return to the goal crease and there is any interference by the attacking player who prevents the goalkeeper from making a play, then the goal must be disallowed and an interference penalty shall be assessed"*, and **Situation 12**, read 31 August 2026, which disallows a goal scored on a one-second rebound with a teammate still in the crease — *"[a]t the time the shot was taken, an attacking player was illegally in the crease"* — and is the nearest thing either amateur book has to an answer on a rebound, though it answers the position bar rather than a contact question — including **Rule 407, Situation 13**, which supplies the determining-factor test the playing rule itself omits: asked whether it is legal for a goalkeeper *"whose skates are beyond the center red line to play the puck which is still on the same side of center red line as the goal they are defending"*, it answers *"No"* and rules that *"in this situation the determining factor is the position of the goalkeeper's skates and not the puck"*, **the opposite of the NHL's and the IIHF's express test**; and **Rule 407, Situation 12**, the accidental case — a goalkeeper on the way to the bench, beyond the red line, *"accidentally struck by the puck"* — answered *"No"*, because *"the goalkeeper is not considered to be participating in the play if the puck contact was accidental."* Those are the only two situations the *Casebook* prints under 407(c). Also **Rule 615, Situations 10, 26 and 27**, which are where USA Hockey works out how 615(e) applies to a goalkeeper during an altercation: Situation 10 assesses *"a minor penalty for leaving the crease, plus a game misconduct for being the first to intervene in an altercation"* (Rule References 615(a, d and e)); **Situation 26** answers *"No"* to a penalty where an altercation occurs in the vicinity of the crease and the goalkeeper leaves *"to remain out of the altercation"*, directing the referee to send them *"to their players' bench or at least away from the altercation"* but adding that *"if the Referee does not give their permission and the goalkeeper gets involved in the altercation outside of the crease, the minor penalty must be assessed"*; and **Situation 27** answers *"Yes"* to whether a goalkeeper may go to their own players' bench when a fight occurs in the immediate vicinity of the crease. Also read: **Appendix VI, the Standard of Play Casebook**, and **Rule 630, Situation 41**, which disallows any goal during a delayed off-side, own goals included and tag-up notwithstanding, and gives its rule reference as 630(d.3), a sub-clause the printed Rule 630(d) does not contain, **and Rule 630, Situation 39**, which directs the linesperson to stop play immediately where offside players *"continue in pursuit of the puck"* because *"[t]he potential for unnecessary body contact should not be allowed in a situation where an infraction (offside) has already occurred"* — the reason the negative above is stated about the **rules** and not about the books — and which carries its own exception where a defender will plainly reach a loose puck first and can advance it out of the zone — together with **Rule 601, Situation 23** (biting a Linesperson), **Rule 602, Situation 3** and **Rule 615, Situation 3** (biting an opponent — a match penalty under 602(a), and during a fight stacked with the fighting major and game misconduct under 615(a)), **Rule 604, Situation 2** (the competitive-contact test), **Rule 605, Situations 1, 2, 3 and 5** with **Rule 301, Situation 6** (the broken stick, the safety purpose the Casebook gives the drop-it rule, and which stick a goalkeeper may take once theirs has gone), **Rule 607, Situation 2** (the charge on an unsuspecting opponent), **Rule 620, Situation 1** (the force needed for head contact), **Rule 639, Situations 1 and 5** (the stumble that never becomes a fall; ducking as clipping, and the floor for slew footing), and — for the stick lift — **Appendix VI, Standard of Play Casebook, Situation 2**, which permits it *"at any time provided they do not impede their progress"* and calls it *"a good defensive play"*, read with **Rule 623, Situation 1**, which first rules that a player who *"hooks the stick of another player causing them to lose possession of the puck"* has committed a penalty. Read in full for a screening or goaltender-vision provision, for a checker's amnesty against an opponent with their head down, and for a crouching clause: it contains none of the three. Where it speaks to the second of those it runs the other way, at Rule 620, Situation 1 and Rule 607, Situation 2. ⚠️ **And it is where USA Hockey's fend-off permission is written**, at **Casebook – Standard of Play, Situation 6**, read 31 August 2026 — *"[a] player who is in control of the puck is allowed to use a free hand to maintain body position that they legally established… the player with the puck uses the free arm to fend off the opponent and maintain their body position. Naturally, though, if they grab the stick, sweater or arm of the opponent, or use the free arm to push off and create space, a penalty should be assessed."* An anchored search of the playing-rules volume returns **no occurrence of "fend"** at all, so searching the playing rules for the NHL's phrase finds nothing in a book that grants the same thing on conditions — search it by concept, or in the Casebook. **Rule 621, Situations 1 to 15** were read in full on 1 September 2026: **Situation 2** answers *"No."* to *"Must contact with an opponent occur in order for the Referee to assess a penalty for high sticking?"* and adds that *"[i]t is not a requirement that a stick make contact above shoulder height for high sticking to be called"*, neither of which the playing rule's 621 Note says; **Situation 1** routes a stick to the head *"during the process of delivering a check"* to Rule 620 Head Contact; **Situation 3** confirms the 621(b) and 621(c) tiers; and **Situations 4 to 15** are all 621(d), high-sticking the puck.*

*Helmet removal in an altercation — the four books, read 1 September 2026: [NHL Official Rules 2025-2026](https://media.d3.nhle.com/image/private/t_document/prd/slwjuaqwmuvj5bkplixo.pdf) — **Rule 46.6 Helmets** (the minor for unsportsmanlike conduct, and *"Helmets that come off in the course of and resulting from the altercation will not result in a penalty to either player"*), with **9.6** and **51.1–51.2**, which are where the roughing minor for removing an opponent's helmet *"during play"* is written · [IIHF Official Rulebook 2025/26 (Version 1.1)](https://blob.iihf.com/iihf-media/iihfmvc/media/contentimages/3_the_iihf/2025-26_iihf_rulebook_22122025-v1.pdf) — **Rule 46.12 Helmets**, and **51.1–51.2** · [IIHF Official Rule Book 2026/27 (Version 1.0)](https://blob.iihf.com/iihf-media/iihfmvc/media/downloads/rule%20book/2026-27_iihf_rule_book.pdf) — **Rule 46.6 Helmets**, the same clause renumbered and reading *"a two minute minor penalty"* where the 2025/26 text reads *"a minor penalty"*; Rule 51 is word for word identical in the two editions, compared 1 September 2026 · [USA Hockey 2025-29 Official Playing Rules](https://cdn2.sportngin.com/attachments/document/945a-3442848/2025-29_USAH_Playing_Rules.pdf) — **Rule 615(c)**, read in full: its four paragraphs are the two match penalties (own helmet *"prior to"*, opponent's *"prior to or during"*), the game misconduct with the two-game suspension, and the game misconduct for a helmet not worn as designed. **Rule 640 was searched for a helmet clause and has none**, so USA Hockey's roughing rule carries no counterpart to NHL and IIHF 51.1 · [USA Hockey Official Rules and Casebook 2025-29](https://cdn3.sportngin.com/attachments/document/4f1a-3442850/2025-29_USAH_Playing_Rules___Casebook.pdf) — **Rule 615, Situations 12 to 21** and the **Note applying to Situations 14-20**, read in full. **Note the lettering, because Rule 610's does not hold here:** the Casebook's printed Rule 615 runs (a) to (f) exactly as the Playing Rules volume does, and its situations cite 615(a), (c), (d) and (e) consistently with it, so the one-letter offset disclosed above for 610 does **not** affect 615. **Two of the book's own Summary of Penalties entries do disagree with each other** — 615(c) is printed under *"Match Penalty Option"* as *"Deliberately removing helmet during altercation"* and again under *"Match Penalty (only)"* as *"Removing helmet prior to or during an altercation"* — so nothing here rests on the summary tables; the tiers above are taken from the rule text and the situations · [Hockey Canada Playing Rules 2026-2028](https://cdn.hockeycanada.ca/hockey-canada/Hockey-Programs/Officiating/Downloads/2026-28-hc-rulebook-e.pdf) — **Rule 10.6 Illegal Equipment**: 10.6(d)'s misconduct for undoing a chinstrap to incite, 10.6(f)(iii) and (iv)'s Gross Misconducts, and **Interpretation 15 to Rule 10.6(f)(iii)(iv)**, which handles the case where nobody removed anything deliberately — no further penalty for an incidental removal, a double minor under 7.1 for grabbing a helmet without gaining an advantage, and a match penalty under 7.1(c)(iv) where it is used *"to gain an advantage or to inflict punishment."* **Rule 4.9(a)** supplies what a Gross Misconduct is. **Rules 7.9 (Roughing) and 7.10 (Fighting) were read for a helmet clause and have none.***

*Rule change history: [Scouting The Refs – NHL Rule Book Updates for 2025-26](https://scoutingtherefs.com/2025/09/49364/nhl-rule-book-updates-for-2025-26/) · [USA Hockey – 2025-29 Playing Rule Change Summary (PDF)](https://cdn3.sportngin.com/attachments/document/f373-3442853/2025-29_Rule_Change_Summary.pdf) — *"Effective August 1, 2025"*; the former `usahockey.com/2025-29-usah-rule-changes` page returned 404 on 21 August 2026 · [USA Hockey – Rule Book & Resources](https://www.usahockey.com/rulesandresources) · [IIHF – New Rule Book for global game](https://www.iihf.com/en/events/2021/wm/news/26308/new_rule_book_for_global_game) — the IIHF's own announcement, 5 June 2021, that it would *"introduce a new Rule Book for the 2021/22 season"*, listing among the rules to be integrated *"A restricted area for goalkeepers behind the net to create more offensive play (i.e. trapezoid familiar to NHL and KHL)"*. **It dates the IIHF's adoption of the trapezoid, and it is the only support held here for the KHL** — a governing body's aside rather than a KHL rule. **Nothing held here dates the NHL's own adoption***

*Icing rule variants and adoption dates: [Icing (ice hockey) — Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Icing_(ice_hockey))*

*Why ducking is dangerous (the anatomy in takeaway 6): [USA Hockey — Heads Up Hockey Program Guide (PDF)](https://cdn4.sportngin.com/attachments/document/0138/6193/HUH_Program_Guide_FINAL.pdf) — "How spinal injuries happen in hockey", refetched and the quoted strings located in the extracted text on 29 August 2026. **The finding beneath the mechanism is a restatement, not USA Hockey's own research:** the page credits *"research done among a wide range of hockey players"* and prints the citation under it — **Charles H. Tator et al., *Spinal Injuries Due To Hockey*, Canadian Journal of Neurological Sciences 11:34–41** — which has not been read in the original here, only the USA Hockey page reproducing it. [Body Contact and Battles](../technique/body_contact_and_battles.md) carries the fuller treatment.*

*Rec-league rules: [CARHA Hockey Official Rule Book, printed 2020 (PDF)](https://carhahockey.ca/wp-content/uploads/2024/01/RuleBk-2020-interactive-1.pdf) — Rules **19(b)** — *"at the players' bench (within 10 ft (3.05 m)) and out of play before any change is made"*, read 31 August 2026 — 34, **40 (Appointment of Officials, read 1 September 2026 — 40(a)'s crew of two Referees, one Official Scorer, one Game Timekeeper, one Penalty Timekeeper and two Goal Judges, and 40(d)'s equal authority and closest-Referee tiebreak; with Rule 41(d), which has the Referees stop play *"for any other infractions of the rules. (e.g. icing the puck, offsides, etc.)"*, and the Glossary entries *"On-Ice Officials"* — *"[t]he Referees"*, no linesperson among them — and *"Off-Ice Officials"*, which between them put five of those seven off the ice. The whole book was searched for *"Linesman"*, *"Linesmen"*, *"linesman"* and *"linesperson"*, raw and whitespace-flattened: **exactly one hit**, the icing signal on p.82 of the Referee's Signals section, which Rule 40 does not provide for)**, **41(e)** (the Referee's final decision on a disputed goal, which is the book's only goal adjudication — it writes no video review and no coach's challenge), 49, **50 (Head Contact) and 62 (High Sticks), read 2 September 2026 — 62 read as a window, because a page banner splits the rule between 62(b) and the Note that escalates it: 62(a)'s minor for a player who *"checks an opponent or intimidates an opponent while carrying their stick above the waist height of their opponent"*, which requires no contact at all and is not a lower rung of (b); 62(b)'s major for contact above the shoulders *"whether or not injury results"*; its Note adding *"a Game Misconduct under Rule 30"* where the contact was intentional and withholding it for an accidental high stick *"except when injury results"*; 62(c)'s match penalty; and 62(d), the puck rule. **Rule 30(a)**, read the same day, is what that Note points at — any major is *"ruled off the ice for the remainder of the game (Major penalty plus Game Misconduct)"*, and its own Note names the accidental high stick as *"[t]he only exception to this rule"* in the book**, **57 (Face-offs, read 1 September 2026 — 57(a)'s ejection of the draw-taker for a teammate's encroachment, and 57(d)'s discretionary minor for a second violation in the same face-off)**, **58(b)** (the goalkeeper's freezing limit, read 1 September 2026 as a line range across a page break; the book carries no trapezoid and no goalkeeper restricted area — searched for *"trapezoid"*, *"restricted area"* and *"privileged"*, all zero, and by concept for any limit on where the goalkeeper may play the puck — so 58(b) limits freezing the puck, not playing it), 59, 65 (including **65(d)**, the shorthanded nullification, unqualified by classification, and Note 2 to 65(a)), 73, 79, 84 (including **84(f)**, the mercy rule) and Section Seven (Tournament General Rules) items 16 and 21, quoted from the rulebook text · [ASHL (Adult Safe Hockey League) Rule Book, Fall 2025 update (PDF)](https://www.ashl.ca/wp-content/uploads/2025/09/ASHL_Rulebook_25FallUpdate_INTERACTIVE.pdf) and [ASHL — Rule Book](https://www.ashl.ca/rule-book/)*

*Unverified: the "typical rec / beer league" column in section 10 remains a generalisation — CARHA Hockey and the ASHL publish real rulebooks and are cited above, but no document governs rec hockey as a whole, and neither book applies to a league that has not adopted it. The length of each overtime period in an IIHF gold-medal game is not stated in IIHF Rule 84.6: the rule specifies the numerical strength (three skaters and a goalkeeper), the 15-minute intermissions and the changing of ends, but gives no period duration, in contrast to Rule 84.5, which sets the playoff, relegation-round, classification-round and bronze-medal overtime at "not more than 10 minutes" and has the clock "reset to 10:00 minutes". The figure is simply not in the book, so none is given.*

*Scope of one negative — the face-off hand bar, 28 August 2026. The note to NHL and IIHF Rule 67.2, printed again at Rule 76.5, suspends the catch-and-knock-it-down permission for the two players taking a draw until a third player has touched the puck. **No counterpart to it was found in USA Hockey or Hockey Canada — but that is a search result, not an established absence.** What was searched: USA Hockey Rule 613 (Face-Off Procedures) and Rule 618 (Handling Puck with Hands), and Hockey Canada Rule 6.2 (Face-off Procedure) with its Interpretation 1, each read in full; then both books flattened to whitespace-normalised text and searched for "third player", "has at least touched", "until such time" and "players involved in the actual"; for every sentence containing both a face-off term and "hand", "bat" or "glove"; for every sentence pairing "centre" or "center" with "hand" or "bat"; and for every sentence naming the players "taking the face-off" or "facing off". Nothing in either book restricts the takers' hands. A counterpart worded without any of those terms would still have escaped, so what is claimed here is that none was found — not that none exists.*

*⚠️ Edition note — the IIHF 2026/27 Rule Book, 29 August 2026. Every IIHF citation above was read in the **IIHF Official Rule Book 2025/26 (Version 1.1)**, which the IIHF has since superseded with the [IIHF Official Rule Book 2026/27 (Version 1.0, June 2026)](https://blob.iihf.com/iihf-media/iihfmvc/media/downloads/rule%20book/2026-27_iihf_rule_book.pdf). **The rules named here and the whole of Appendix IV have been compared between the two editions for this document — 20.4, 20.5, 20.6, the whole of Rule 46, 60.1, 60.2, 60.3, 60.4, 60.5, the whole of 63.2, 69.4, 69.7, 76.3, 76.4, 76.6, 76.7, 78.5(IX), 81.4, 82.1, 82.2, 83.1 and 87.1 — and nothing else in the new book has been read.** **69.4 is word for word the same in both editions**, its conditional permission included, and so are **69.7**, all four of its paragraphs, and the **78.5(IX)** cross-reference to it. Rule 76 has changed substantially, and section 8 and the face-off row of the section 10 table are written to the new edition: 76.6 now ejects the offending team's draw-taker from the circle for a teammate's encroachment and has the linesperson order the centre replaced on the establish-position-first violation, 76.4 has the player ordered replaced for improper position and has **gained** the NHL's post-icing carve-out that the older edition had no need of, 76.3(VI) now has the offending centre replaced for a skate win rather than warned, and 76.7 has dropped its warning sentence. The skate-win violation itself, the stick-placement order in 76.4 and the two-violation bench minor in 76.7 are unchanged. **Rule 60 has changed too, and the high-sticking material in section 8, the section 10 row and the Common Mistakes list is written to both editions rather than one:** the new 60.1 defines a high stick by *contact* above the shoulders *"provided their shoulders are at waist level or higher"* where the older one defines it by a stick *"carried above the height of the opponent's shoulders"*, and the new 60.3 carries *"(above the height of the opponent's waist)"* where the older one requires that the Player *"carries or holds any part of their stick above the shoulders and"* makes contact. **60.2 and 60.4 are word-for-word identical between the editions**; 60.5 gains a Note on the penalty shot that the NHL book also carries. The word "waist" appears **once** in the 2025/26 book and **four** times in the 2026/27 one, both counts taken on text normalised for hyphenated line breaks. **Rule 46 and Appendix IV are renumbered, and the Rule 46 numbers above are the 2025/26 book's.** The 2026/27 book reorders Rule 46's clauses and **eleven** numbers move: instigator 46.3 becomes 46.10, aggressor 46.4 becomes 46.2, sucker punch 46.5 becomes 46.13, third player in 46.7 becomes 46.14, fighting other than during the periods 46.9 becomes 46.8, fighting off the playing surface 46.11 becomes 46.7, altercation 46.2 becomes 46.3, clearing the area of a fight 46.8 becomes 46.4, helmets 46.12 becomes 46.6, fighting prior to the drop of the puck 46.10 becomes 46.9, and jerseys 46.13 becomes 46.11. The new order is roughly alphabetical by clause heading but breaks at least three times — helmets at 46.6 between *continuing* and *fighting off the playing surface*, dangerous punch at 46.13 after *major penalty*, and disciplinary measures last at 46.15 — so a number cannot be predicted from a heading. ⚠️ **46.1 also changed in substance, and it inverts the default:** the 2025/26 clause says a willing combatant *"may be ejected from the game"*, where the 2026/27 clause makes a major plus an automatic game misconduct *"the standard penalty for a Player engaged in a fight"* and a new 46.12 repeats it. The standalone unwilling-combatant clause at 46.6 is gone, carried in the new book as that discretionary waiver note inside four other clauses — and with it the 2025/26 sentence that a player *"who retaliates to being punched will be assessed at least a minor penalty ('Roughing') or a major penalty ('Fighting', without a game misconduct penalty)"*, the new book carrying no retaliation ladder at all; the sucker-punch injury test narrowed from a punch *"that could cause an injury"* (46.5) to one that *"causes an injury"* (46.13), though the penalty is a major plus an automatic game misconduct either way; Rule 20.6's video-review exception follows the move and now names 46.13 rather than 46.5; and the third-player-in waiver is **narrower** in the new book, keyed to an aggressor penalty plus a purpose test rather than to any game misconduct being imposed in the original altercation. **The Appendix tables move with it: the 2026/27 book has no Table 6.** Its predecessor's Tables 5, 6 and 7 are merged into a single Table 5 with *"MAJOR ONLY"* and *"MAJOR + GMP"* columns, carrying every entry the old three did plus two fighting rows they did not; but the new book's own 20.4 still ends *"refer to… Table 6"* and its 20.5 still says *"Tables 5, 6 and 7"*, and *"Table 6"* appears exactly once in the whole book — in that dead cross-reference. The text of 20.4 itself is word-for-word identical between the editions. ⚠️ **The merge shifts every table below it down by two, so the numbers cited above are lookups into the older book:** misconduct penalties 9 becomes 7, game misconducts 10 becomes 8, penalty shot 11 becomes 9, awarded goals 12 and 12.1 become 10 and 10.1, the goalkeeper's penalties 13 becomes 11, Rule 16 minors 14 becomes 12, coincidental penalties 15 becomes 13, **interference on the goalkeeper 16 becomes 14**, and the two overtime tables 17 and 18 become 15 and 16. **So Table 16 in the current book is Rule 84 Overtime, not the goalkeeper table this document cites at Table 16.** The goalkeeper table itself is unchanged in substance: set side by side and normalised for whitespace, the two run to the same 202 lines and differ only in the running header, so Situations 5C, 6C and 6D read identically in both editions. **Rules 81.4, 82.1, 82.2 and 87.1 are word-for-word identical between the editions**, broken-skate exception and time-out bans included, so the icing rows of the section 10 table are current — **including the mismatch between 81.4's four substitution exceptions and 82.1's three, which both editions carry unchanged.** ⚠️ **83.1 is the rule compared here that changed most in what it asks of a player, and it changed toward the NHL.** The 2026/27 edition adds *"For the purposes of this rule, a 'skate' is to be considered the blade of the skate only"*, which the 2025/26 book does not carry, and rewrites the puck-carrier clause from *"provided they have 'possession and control' of the puck prior to their skates crossing the leading edge"* to *"…of the puck with their stick prior to both skates completely crossing the leading edge"* — the NHL 2025-2026 wording in both places. On text flattened for hyphenated line breaks, *"blade of the skate"* appears **0** times in the 2025/26 book and **once** in the 2026/27 one, and *"prior to their skates crossing the leading edge"* **once** and **0**. **Only 83.1 was compared in full**; inside 83.3 just the *"'3D' judgement is not used"* clearing sentence was checked, and it is unchanged. **So is the whole of 63.2**, bench clause included: (III) still names only *"their hand or stick"* and (II) still reaches a *"hand, leg, foot, arm, stick etc."* from anywhere on the ice — while the Situation Handbook answers the kicked puck *"Yes"* at 63.19 in both of its last two editions and at 63.34 in all three, as above. **No claim is made here about the rest of this document's IIHF layer** — Rules 9, 10, the rest of 20, 22, 23, 24, 27, 37, 38, 40 to 45, 47 to 59, 61, 62, the rest of 63, the rest of 69, the rest of 83 and 84 have not been re-read in the new edition, and the 2026/27 Situation Handbook has been read for Situations 60.13, 63.3, 63.19, 63.24, 63.28, 63.29 and 63.34 only: [IIHF Situation Handbook 2026/27 (Version 1.0, June 2026)](https://blob.iihf.com/iihf-media/iihfmvc/media/downloads/officiating%20files/situation%20handbook/2026-27_iihf_situation_handbook.pdf), which puts 63.19 in the same facts and answers *"Yes."* again, and words 63.3, 63.24, 63.28, 63.29 and 63.34 as the 2025/26 edition does. **Situation 60.13, the stick lift that misses and catches a bent-over opponent in the face, has no counterpart in the 2025/26 Handbook, whose Rule 60 section ends at Situation 60.12.** [Faceoffs](../systems/faceoffs.md) carries the full face-off comparison.*

*⚠️ In-House note — the IHUK In-House Rules 2026-27, 29 August 2026. **IHUK has published a new edition of the In-House Rules since 2025-26, and it settles the British face-off answer in its own text.** [IHUK In-House Rules 2026-27, Revision 1](https://englandicehockey.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/IHUK-In-House-Rules-2026-2027.pdf) was published on 25 July 2026 and has been read. **Three amendments in it are new — none has any counterpart in the 2025-26 edition, which was re-searched** — and all three are quoted in full above: the *Section 10* Rule 76 face-off amendment (immediate replacement of the centre for all violations except on post-icing draws, and *"Either centre can now put their stick down first for a face-off"*), the *Section 9* Rule 64 embellishment amendment (*"The required call is now 2 plus 10"*), and the *Section 3 – Equipment* Rule 10.3 broken-stick amendment (*"Skating with a broken stick anywhere on the ice is not permitted under IHUK In-House Rules"*, a *"2min penalty for illegal equipment"*). The word *"broken"* does not appear at all in the 2025-26 edition. **The broken-stick amendment is headed *"Broken Stick: Player"* and IHUK writes no goalkeeper counterpart to it**, so nothing above reads it onto a goalkeeper. **The stick-order change is IHUK's own departure, not an IIHF change:** IIHF Rule 76.4's defending-player-first sentence is word-for-word identical in the 2025/26 and 2026/27 editions, and both were checked. ⚠️ **What this does not settle is which IIHF edition British hockey sits on.** The 2026-27 cover page reads *"the **Current** IIHF Rule Book"* where its predecessor named the 2025–26 book, but its Introduction still adopts *"the latest IIHF Official Rule Book (Version 1.1, published July 2025)"* — Version 1.1 of July 2025 being the **2025/26** book, the 2026/27 one giving its own version as 1.0, June 2026. **The document contradicts itself, so nothing here treats it as evidence either way**, and the face-off answer above does not depend on it. ⚠️ **Scope:** the In-House Rules govern the game *"in England, Wales, Scotland and Northern Ireland (the Home Countries)"*; the Rule 76, Rule 64 and Rule 10.3 amendments carry no league restriction of their own, where the Section 6 fighting provision is headed *"Applicable Leagues: NIHL (all leagues), SNL, and BUIHA Varsity"*; and **the Elite League is not mentioned anywhere in the document**, so nothing above speaks to an EIHL game. **One further provision was read and is deliberately not carried into the text above**: the 2026-27 Section 4 preamble instructs officials to label penalties precisely, including that a cross-check to the head or neck *"be called Cross Checking, not Checking to the Head."* That is a direction about which label an official writes down; it changes nothing about what is illegal, and it is left out rather than summarised because a one-sentence version of it read aloud would be dangerous.*
